4. (He should utter) Om after putting his hands upon the feet (left hand upon the right foot and the right hand upon the left hand) and putting the Pavitras (Darbha-grass) in the hands. Yajus (Om) is the representative of knowledge of the three Vedas; this is the speech, this is the Great one, not to be destroyed.

5. As to that it has been said by this Rc.

6. The Rces are in the great protecting Aksara (not destroyable) Om, in which all the gods sit; who does not know that, what will he do by the Rces? Who, verily, know that they are sitting here.

7. (One) uses only the three Vedas; he says Bhur, Bhuvah, Svar; this is the truth of the speech; which is the truth of the speech, he uses that.

8. He should read thrice the Gayatri Mantra of Savitr-god, at first (stopping) on the words, then (stopping) on the half-Rces and then in a breath (not stopping anywhere in the middle). The god Savitr is the impeller of the prosperity; he obtains, indeed, the prosperity. He should begin the verses (on the next day) after the read words (1).

[[P144]]

XII

1. (One) should read his lesson silently in a village.

2. Ahneys, the son of Suci has said thus.

3. And in the forest, if he is unable (to read properly) he must read the lesson by uttering, standing, moving, sitting or laying; knowing thus, who reads the lesson, he becomes a pious ascetic.

4. Salutation to Brahman, may salutation be for the fire, salutation to the earth, salutation to the plants, salutation to the Vac, salutation to the Vacaspati; I make salutation to Visnu, the great one (1).

XIII

Note: Rules for reading in the village and the forest and the salutations. (From 1 to 5). Other particulars of Brahma-Yajna, with quotation and Brahmana-passage.

1. (One) should read powerfully at the mid-day; that Brahmana is, verily, the sun (Aditya); hence the sun has much heat at that time. As to that this has been said:—

[[P145]]

2. Wonderful army of the divine ones, the eye of Mitra, Varuna and Agni has risen up. He fulfils the heaven, the earth and Surya (sun) is the soul of all the moving and the fixed ones.

3. This sacrifice (Brahma-Yajna) is always spread freshly and finished freshly; at the evening is the finishing of that sacrifice (Brahma-Yajna), begun in the morning.

4. He utters the concluding Mantra “Salutation to Brahman…” thrice.

5. After touching the waters he comes to his home; whatever he gives there, that is the Daksina (sacrificial fee) (1).

XIV

Note: Study in the rain etc. and its fruit. (From 1 to 3).

1. Of this sacrifice the cloud is the oblation oracle; the lightning, the fire; the rain, the oblation; the roaring of the cloud, the Vasatkara; the roaring of the lightning, the following Vasatkara; the wind, the soul; and the Amavasya, the offering of the right sacrifice.

[[P146]]

2. He, who knowing this, reads his lesson in the rain, in the flash of the lightning, in the roaring of the cloud, in the roaring of the lightning, in the wind and in the Amavasya, that is performed, verily, an austerity; for the reading of one’s lesson is, verily, an austerity.

3. (He) ascends the supreme heaven, becomes supreme among his equals; (he) obtains that great and not returning world which a person obtains after donating this earth full of the wealth; he wins the death, which comes again and again; he obtains the intimate relation with the Brahman (1).

XV

Note: Intermissions of study and the details and importance of the Brahma-Yajna. (From 1 to 9).

1. There are, verily, two intermissions of study when he is not pure and when the place is not pure.

2. (In this sacrifice) the gods are the materials (of the sacrifice).

3. Who, knowing thus, reads his lesson according to his power, in the midnight, at the time of the dawn, moving, standing, sitting, laying, in the forest or in the village: he wins all the worlds, he moves in all the worlds debtless. As to that this has been said:—

4. May we be debtless in this, debtless in that, debtless in the third world. Whatever are the ways to the gods or to the fathers (Devayanas and Pitryanas) may we, the debtless, obtain them.

5. The sin caught the born fire; the gods destroyed that sin by the oblations; they destroyed the sin of the oblations by the sacrifice; of the sacrifice, by the Daksina (sacrificial fee); of the Daksina, by the Brahmana; of the Brahmana, by the Mantras; of the Mantras, by study of one’s own lesson; the study of one’s own lesson is, verily, sinless, divinely pure; who leaves his study, he becomes shareless in the speech, shareless in the heaven; as to that this has been said:—

[[P147]]

6. He, who abandoned his friend (the study), who knows friendship, has not a part even in the speech, whatever he hears, hears in vain; he does not know that path of the good deed.

7. Therefore, one should read his lesson; whatever sacrifice he reads, by that all it is sacrificed for him; he obtains the intimate relation with Fire, Wind, and Sun; as to that this has been said:—

8. Who abuse the new or old readers (learned ones), the knowers of the Vedas; they all abuse the sun, the fire, the second and the swan (Hamsa = Atman), the third.

9. Whatever gods there are, they all live in the Brahmana, who knows the Vedas. Therefore, (one) should salute daily the Brahmanas, who know the Vedas; should not use bad words for them; (by this way) he pleases these gods (living in the Brahmanas) (1).

XVI

1. He, who causes (ones) sacrifice and who takes (sacrificial fee) from them, becomes empty in this and in that world. After causing one sacrifice and taking (fee) from him, he should read thrice his lesson of the Veda without taking the meal.

2. Or he should read for three nights the Gayatri Mantra of the god Savitr; in this way he makes himself not empty.

3. The cow (vara) is the Daksina (sacrificial fee); he pleases vara (Atman) by the vara (cow); for the Atman is the vara (1).

XVII

Note: Atonement by study. (From 1 to 3).

1. He milks out the Mantras, who causes (one) sacrifice. By which Mantras of the sacrifice he causes (one) sacrifice, he should read that lesson going in the forest, sitting in a pure place.

[[P148]]

2. Not eating is his consecration; the place, the Upasada (going near); the seat, the pressing of the Soma; the speech, the oblation; the mind, the Upabhrt (the sacrificial ladle); the resolution, the Dhruva (the sacrificial ladle); the breath (Prana), the sacrificing; the Saman, the Adhvaryu priest. This sacrifice is of the Daksina (fee) of the breaths, the eternal Daksina, which is more prosperous (1).

XVIII

Note: Atonement for the violation of the vow of chastity. (From 1 to 8).

1. In how many ways (one), who has violated his vow of chastity, enters (the gods)? The speakers of the sacred knowledge say: in four ways, by the breaths to Maruts, by the vigours to Indra, by the splendor of the sacred knowledge to Brhaspati and by the all others to Fire. Sudeva, the son of Kasyapa, has found out this atonement of that.

2. Which student of the sacred knowledge violates the vow of chastity, he sacrifices two separate oblations of ghee after bringing and keeping the fire (by the Mantra) “O Kama (god of the desire and love etc.), I have violated the vow of chastity, O Kama, I have violated the vow of chastity; hail for Kama; O Kama, I have committed the offence, O Kama; hail for Kama.

Nectar is, verily, the ghee; he puts in himself the ghee, the nectar.

3. After sacrificing, putting together his palms of the hands, he should utter these Mantras standing in the sideway of the fire:—

[[P149]]

4. May Maruts make me full again, may Indra make me full again, may Brhaspati make me full again, may this fire make me full again by life and vigour; may they make me full of life.

5. Maruts put breaths (Pranas) in him, Indra puts vigour in him, Brhaspati puts the splendor of the sacred knowledge in him, the fire puts all others in him, becoming full in all the limbs of the body he obtains the whole (span of) life.

6. He should utter the Mantras thrice, for the gods are thrice (fully) true.

7. He, who thinks himself impious, should sacrifice thus, utter the Mantra thus; he makes (in this way) purified himself; he puts the life in himself.

8. The cow (vara) is the Daksina (sacrificial fee); he pleases the vara (Atman) by vara; for Atman is the vara.

XIX

Note: Salutations and the description of a divine dolphin. (From 1 to 5).

1. I obtain Bhuh (the earth), I obtain the Bhuvah (the mid-region), I obtain the Svar (the heaven), I obtain the Brahman, I obtain the sheath of the Brahman, I obtain the nectar, I obtain the sheath of the nectar, which the death does not see, I obtain that, I obtain the gods, I obtain the town of the gods by that Brahma, by that armour being covered and recovered by the brilliance of Kasyapa.

[[P150]]

2. To whom we salute, he is the head; the religion, the skull; the Brahman, the upper jaw; the sacrifice, the lower jaw; Visnu, the heart; the Year, the generative organ; Asvins, the back feet; Atri, the middle body; Mitra and Varuna, the front feet; the fire, the first part of the tail: then Indra, the second, then Prajapati, the third and the not fearing one, the fourth part of the tail (these are the limbs of the divine dolphin).

3. Such is that powerful divine dolphin; to that:-

4. Who knows thus, he wins the death which comes again and again; he wins the heavenly world; he does not die in the way; he does not die in the fire; he does not die in the waters; he does not die without offsprings; he obtains the food easily.

5. You are firm (polar star), you are the residing place of the firm (worlds), you are lord of the beings, you are supreme among the beings; the beings serve you from all sides; salutation to you, salutation, all salutation to you, salutation to Sisukumara (dolphin) (1).

XX

Note: Salutations. (From 1 to 3).

1. Salutation to the eastern quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter.

2. Salutation to the southern quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter; salutation to the western quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter; salutation to the northern quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter; salutation to the aboveward quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter; salutation to the belowward quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter; salutation to the intermediate quarter and salutation, salutation to those gods, who live in this quarter.

[[P151]]

3. Salutation to those, who live between the Ganga and the Yamuna; may they, pleasant-minded ones increase our life; salutation to the seers, who reside between the Ganga and the Yamuna; salutation, salutation to the seers, who reside between the Ganga and the Yamuna (1).

Salutation to Brahman, may salutation be to Fire, salutation to earth; salutation to the plants, salutation to Vāc (speech), salutation to Vacaspati (lord of speech), I make salutation to great Visnu.

Om Peace!

Peace!

Peace!

[[P152]]

III

[[P153]]

I

Note: Mantras for peace and propitiation.

Harih Om.

I pray for that happiness and welfare for the progress of the sacrifice (and) for the progress of the sacrificer. May the divine welfare be for us, (so that) no sufferings take place. May propitiation be for our two-footed (men); may propitiation be for the four-footed (cattle). Further, may the medicine (remover of all) take place.

Om Peace! Peace! Peace!

1. Om. The thinking is the Sruc (large wooden ladle); the heart, the ghee; the Vac (speech), the altar; the uttering, the Barhi-grass; the Hotṛ, the fire; knowledge, (also) the fire; the lord of speech (Vacaspati), the invoker; the mind, the Upavakṭr (the impeller); the breath, the oblation; the Samana, the Adhvaryu priest.

2. O Vacaspati (lord of speech), O disposer, making others bend (to salute), we spread your fame; spread our fame; may Vacaspati drink Soma and may he put vigour in us; hail! (1)

II

Note: Mantras of the Caturhotra Citi. Identification of the priests with the earth etc.

1. The earth is the Hotṛ priest; the heaven, the Adhvaryu priest; Rudra, verily, the fire; Bṛhaspati, the impeller.165

2. O Vacaspati, you are worshipped by the full power of the speech; grant the heaven, which is worth choosing, for the sacrificer; Vacaspati drinks the Soma-juice; may he generate Indra for the sake of the power of the senses (1).

[[P154]]

III

1. The fire is the Hotṛ priest; Aśvins, the Adhvaryu priest; Tvaṣṭr, verily, the fire; Mitra, the impeller.

2. Soma is the first going one of the Soma-plant; the white Soma-juice is the first going one of the white (shining Mantras). Indra, Soma-juices have been prepared for you, who are of the speed of the wind and the hearer of the invokings; hail! (1)

IV

1. The sun is your eye; the wind, the breath; the heaven, the back; the mid-region, the soul; (obtain) the sacrifice by the limbs; the earth, by the bodies.

2. O Vacaspati, by the full speech, by the unbroken ladle impel the sacrifice which increases the gods, in the heaven (1).

V

Note: Mantras describing the seven seers identified with the priests etc.

1. (The seer) Mahāhavi is the Hotṛ priest; Satyasavi, the Adhvaryu priest; Acyutatejas, verily, the fire; Acyutamanas, the impeller; Anadhravis and Apratadhravis, the Abhigar priests (who call out approvingly to the other priests); Acyutasya, the Udgātṛ priest.166

[[P155]]

2. O Vacaspati, O disposer of the heart, making others bend (to salute), I spread your fame; spread our fame. Vacaspati drank the Soma-juice. Do not cut the divine line, do not cut the manly line. Salutation to the heaven, salutation to the earth; hail! (1)

VI

1. The speech is the Hotṛ priest; the consecration, the wife; the wind, the Adhvaryu priest; the waters, the Abhigar priest; the mind, the oblation; I sacrifice in the austerity.

2. Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Suvaḥ. Brahman is self-born, salutation to the Brahman, the self-born (1).

VII

Note: Bodily functions identified with the priests, etc. and prayer. Ten Mantras to be uttered after the Cāturmāsya-Yāga. (From 1 to 10).

1. Brahman is the sacrificer of one (sacrificing by his maturity only): he is the sacrifice; may he grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may he become my sacrifice.167

[[P156]]

2. The fire is the sacrificer of the two (being useful in cooking and carrying the oblations); that is the nourisher; may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may that become my nourisher.

3. The earth is the sacrificer of three (being the place of the fire, food and sacrifice); that is the firm-standing (1); may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may that become my firm-standing.

4. The mid-region is the sacrificer of the four (being the causer of speech, rain, space and place); that is the disposer in various ways; may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may that become disposer in various ways for me.

5. The wind is the sacrificer of five (five breaths); that is the breath; may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may that become my breath (2).

6. The moon is the sacrificer of six (i.e., the seasons); that causes the seasons; may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may the seasons be for me.

7. The food is the sacrificer of seven (seven holes of the head); that is the breath of the breath; may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may that become the breath of my breath.

8. The heaven is the sacrificer of eight (eight suns); that is one not to be injured (3); may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may I become one not to be injured.

9. Aditya is the sacrificer of the nine (eight suns and Aditya); that is brilliant; may that grant me offspring, cattle, fame; and may I become brilliant.

[[P157]]

10. Prajāpati is the sacrificer of ten (nine holes of the body and the navel), he is this all; may he grant me offspring, cattle, fame, and may all be mine (4).

VIII

1. The Fire (becomes satisfied) by the Yajuses; Savitṛ (sun), by the hymns; Indra, by the praises; Mitra and Varuṇa, by the prayers; Āṅgirases, by the Dhāniyāte (of side-altar) fires; Maruts, by the seat of the oblation-receptacles; the waters, by the prokṣaṇīs (vessels for putting the holy water); the plants, by the Barhi-grass; Aditi, by the altar; Soma, by the consecration (1); Tvaṣṭṛ, by the fuel; Viṣṇu, by the sacrifice; Vasus, by the ghee; Ādityas, by the Dakṣiṇā (sacrificial fee); All-gods (Viśvedevān), by the power; Pūṣan, by giving (their) own (oblations to the gods); Bṛhaspati, by the priest; Prajāpati, by the Udgītha (singing Samans in particular modes); the mid-region, by the Pavitra (fringed filtering cloth); the wind, by the pots; and I, by the faithfulness (2).

IX

Note: The Śambhāra Yajus Mantras. The wives of the gods.

1. Senā (army) is (the wife) of Indra; Dhenu (speech), of Bṛhaspati; Pathyā (salutary one), of Pūṣan; Vāk (speech), of the wind; Dīkṣā (consecration), of Soma; the earth, of the fire; Gāyatrī (-Mantra), of Vasus; Triṣṭup (-Mantra), of Rudras; Jagatī (-Mantra), of the Ādityas; Anuṣṭup (-Mantra), of Viṣṇu (1); Virāṭ (-Mantra), of Varuṇa; Paṅkti (-Mantra), of the sacrifice; Anumati (assent), of Prajāpati; Śraddhā (faithfulness), of Mitra; Prasūti (Impelling), of Savitṛ; Marīci (ray), of Sūrya (sun); Rohiṇī (-constellation), of Candramas (moon); Arundhatī (-constellation), of the seers (Ṛṣis); the lightning, of Parjanya; the four quarters, the four sub-quarters, the day and the night, the agriculture and the rain, the light and the compensation, the waters and the plants; the vigour and the joy—these are the wives of the gods (2).

[[P158]]

X

Note: Mantras for taking Dakṣiṇā (sacrificial fee).

1. O Goddess Dakṣiṇā (Sacrificial Fee), I take you by the impelling of the impeller Savitṛ, by the hands of Aśvins, by the hands of Pūṣan; may Varuṇa, the king carry you to the fire as gold; by that may we obtain immortality; may this (Dakṣiṇā) be food for the donator and comfort for me, the obtainer. Kāma (desire or cupid) gave it for Kāma; Kāma is the donator (1); Kāma is the obtainer. (O Dakṣiṇā) enter Kāma; I take you by Kāma (desire); O Kāma, this is yours; O Kāma, this is your Dakṣiṇā (sacrificial fee). (O Dakṣiṇā), may Uttāna Āṅgirasa obtain you.

2. O goddess Dakṣiṇā, I take you by the impelling of Savitṛ, by the hands of Aśvins, by the hands of Pūṣan; may Varuṇa, the king carry you for Soma as clothes, for Rudra as cow, for Varuṇa as horse, for Prajāpati as Puruṣa (person) (2), for Manu as bed, for Tvaṣṭr as goat, for Pūṣan as sheep, for poverty as mule and ass, for the Himavant as elephant, for Gandharvas and Apsarases as garland and ornaments, for all gods as food, for speech as corn, for Brahman as boiled rice, for the ocean as waters (3), for Uttāna Āṅgirasa as carriage, for Vaiśvānara (fire) as chariot.

[[P159]]

3. Vaiśvānara ascended in the heaven as ancient one; that upper place of the heaven is welfare-doing one by the good hymns; that Vaiśvānara, producing wealth for the beings moves all round uniformly as a career, an awaking one.

4. O Dakṣiṇā (Sacrificial Fee), may the King Varuṇa carry you as the chariot for Vaiśvānara; by that may we obtain the immortality; may food be for the donator and comfort for me, the obtainer (4). Who gave it whom? Kāma (desire or cupid) gave it for Kāma; Kāma is the donator, Kāma is the obtainer; (O Dakṣiṇā) enter Kāma; I take you by Kāma (desire); O Kāma, this is yours; O Kāma, this is your Dakṣiṇā (sacrificial fee); may Uttāna Āṅgirasa obtain you (5).168

XI

Note: Hotṛ-hṛdaya Mantras.

1. I know well the soul (Ātman) of Indra, which is (like) the gold, shining and longing, moving in ten ways; moving in the ocean (waters) by his mind (to create the worlds). Brahmā knew that in the ocean. He is the ruler of the men, entered their hearts; He is one going on in various forms. Where a hundred lights become one, where all the Vedas become one, where all the Hotṛs become one; that is the Self (Ātman) of the men living in their minds (1), the ruler of the men, entering their hearts. Where all born ones become one, where the four Hotṛs (priests) obtain the prosperity, that is the Self (Ātman) of the men, living in their minds.169

[[P160]]

2. Brahmā knew by the austerity that (Ātman as) Indra, the firm-standing of the worlds, the soul of the heaven, Savitṛ and Bṛhaspati, the four Hotṛs pervading the quarters, the vigour of the speech.170

3. The poets (sages) knew decidedly that Ātman, entered the inner heart, (in the form of) Tvaṣṭr, spreading the forms, the knowing one (2), the breath of the immortality, this sacrifice, and the soul of four Hotṛs (priests).

4. The poets (sages) knew decidedly this Ātman, entered the inner heart, the brother of the gods, put in the minds and this sacrifice, performed by the immortality.

5. This Ātman of Indra, the sacrificer of the five, living everywhere, worth choosing by all (in the form of the wind) knows all, hundred millions; he pervades all the creation; he is the nectar of the gods and the life of the men.171

6. The poets (sages) decidedly knew this Indra, the king, Savitṛ, the Self (Ātman) of the mind; the poets protect (see) always in the foot of the Ṛta (Settled Order) (that Ātman as) the ray, being heated in the rays.

[[P162]]

7. Who holds the world in the sheath of the egg of the creation (Aṇḍakośa) having no difference with that; after that (breaking the sheath) who sees the worlds, whose power has been called this sheath of the egg of the creation; and whose membrane has been called breath; I have been formed by him, by that Immortality.

8. (The learned ones) describe that Ātman as gold (moon),172 sheath, covered by the regions, the treasure of the gods, shining well (4), a part of the sacrificer of the six (of the moon) full of the nectar; the (others) do not know that.173

9. By whom the seasons have been made five-fold or even six-fold by the mind; the sages see that sacrificer of the six, causing the seasons in the foot of the Ṛta (Settled Order).

10. The gods, though living with this, do not know this acting Ātman, entered the inner heart, moving in the moon by the mind, Self (Ātman) of Indra, moving in hundred ways (5).

11. Indra, the king, who rules the world, is the sacrificer of the seven, caused seven-fold.174175

12. Brahmā knew the heart of the gods, being its line sprinkled by the Supreme One, moving in Āditya by the mind.

13. Brahmā took up this (sacrificer of the seven) from Brahman as the sun, raining, in the midst of the waters (of the clouds).

14. In whom (Āditya) seven protectors (rays) rain the abundant prosperity: O Indra (give us that prosperity) full of many horses and cows (6).

[[P163]]

15. That Hari, the best obtainer of the wealth and the protector sprinkles that prosperity for Indra which is not destroyable, full of abundance.

16. May Indra give us that prosperity which is abundant, not destroyable, full of many horses and cows.

17. There are ten thousands of his (Āditya’s) rays; may Indra (Āditya) as ray come to us being increased by our prayers; may he, the ray, Savitṛ, Indra grant us much wealth (7).

18. May this fire put in me ghee, brilliance, honeyful sense-organ.

19. Who moves in the sky as Hari, Pataga (sun), Patari (full of shining folds), Suparṇa (divine bird), dweller of the heaven; may that Indra (Āditya = sun) grant us the desired boon.

20. There moves the great wheel (year) of five fellies (seasons); that golden light (Āditya) goes creeping slowly in the sky, in the middle of the waters of the clouds, as great light; may that Indra (Āditya) grant me the desired boon.

21. Seven (rays) move the chariot of one (wheel) (8); (in reality) one horse, having seven names, carries that wheel of three navels (three main seasons, i.e., cool, hot and rainy), which is not destroyable and sinless and by which all these worlds live.

22. The seers, the knowers of the heaven, seeing this auspicious (Āditya) performed austerity and consecration; then there was born the Kṣatriya (caste), power and vigour; may the gods give this sacrificer (that all).

23. They decidedly knew this (Āditya) in the highest heaven as the white ray, being eaten by the beings again and again; as the carrier of the waters; as the protector and as Indra (ruler) (9).

[[P164]]

24. The red and the yellow waters (become) one (-coloured yellow in the clouds); these yellow waters of one form falling (on the earth) become ten thousands and lakhs and of the needs of the boats.

25. Which is this white Ray, that is all this world; may that give us offspring, cattle and wealth.

26. The White Ray has become all; may that impel for me various cattle.

27. By the power of the Almighty the seers see the clear sun176 by their heart; the other poets see them in the midst of the ocean (sky); the wise ones wish the foot of the rays.

28. The sun (Āditya) holds the speech by the mind; the wind utters that in the body; the sages protect that shining, heaven-giving speech in the foot of the heaven.

29. Whatever are the cattle of the village having many forms, having various forms, many and (as cattle) having one form; may the fire release them for us first (11); and may Prajāpati, concordant by his progeny, (grant them for us).

30. (O Agni and Prajāpati), grant producing power in our all cattle and increase this sacrificer.

31. Whatever are the cattle of the village having all forms, having various forms, many and (as cattle) having one form; may the play of them all seven be here for wealth, nourishing and good vitality.

32. Whatever are the cattle of the forest having many forms, having various forms, many and (as cattle) having one form; may the wind release them for us first; and may Prajāpati, concordant by his progeny, (grant them for us).

[[P165]]

33. The gods knew the fixed and the moving world born of the food like the ghee put in the cave (of their heart).

34. Whatever are the cattle of the forest, having many forms, having various forms, many and (as cattle) having one form; may the play of them all seven be here for wealth, nourishing and good vitality (12).

XII

Note: The Puruṣa Sūkta. (From 1 to 18).

1. Hundred-headed Puruṣa (Person) having hundred eyes, hundred feet, after covering all the worlds stood larger (than these) by ten fingers.

2. That all is Puruṣa (only), whatever was (before) or whatever will be (further); He is the Lord of the immortality (gods) as well as of those, who are born (living on) by the food.

3. This is His greatness; Puruṣa (Person) Himself is greater than this (1); all the beings are His (one) foot; His other three feet are in the heaven (in the form of) nectar.

4. Puruṣa of three feet rises above; His feet is also here again; then He pervaded various ones who eat or who do not eat (as mountains etc.).

5. Of Him Virāṭ was born; of Virāṭ a person was born; after being born he became (various) other than Virāṭ; after him the earth and the bodies were born (2).

6. When the gods performed the sacrifice by the oblation of the person, then Vasant (spring season) was its ghee, Grīṣma (summer) was fuel and Śarad (autumn) was the oblation.

[[P166]]

7. Seven were the Paridhis (enclosures) of this and twenty-one were the fuels, when the gods bound the person as cattle (for sacrificing).

8. They sprinkled in Barhi-grass (by mind) that person, born before (the other beings) (3); by him the gods, the sages and the seers sacrificed.

9. From that sacrifice, where all (in the form of the person) was sacrificed came forth the wonderful ghee and that produced those cattle who are related to the wind, who are wild and who are of the village (tame).

10. From that sacrifice, where all (in the form of the person) was sacrificed, came forth the Ṛcas, the Sāmans, the verses came forth from that, the Yajus was produced from that (4).

11. From that the horses were produced and also those, who have teeth in the sides of the mouth (as asses etc.) were born; the cows came forth from that; the goats and the sheep were born of him.

12. In how many ways they caused the person, when they caused the person? What is called his mouth? What are the hands? What are the thighs? And what are called the legs?

13. Brāhmaṇa (caste) was his mouth; Kṣatriya was his hands (5); Vaiśya was his thighs and Śūdra was born of the legs.

14. The moon was born of (his) mind; the sun was born of the eye, Indra and the fire were born of the mouth and the wind was born of the breath.

15. From the navel the mid-region was born; from the head moved round the heaven; from the legs the earth and from the ear the quarters came forth; in that way they caused all the world (6).

[[P168]]

16. I know this Great Puruṣa, who has the colour of the sun, who is out of the darkness; He, the wise one, is merciful on the world after deciding all the forms and giving the names to them.

17. Whom the disposer made famous, whom Indra, knowing all the quarters, made famous; by knowing Him one becomes immortal here; there is no other way for going to the (Supreme).

18. The gods performed the sacrifice by the sacrifice (sacrificial person); these were the first holders (Dharma) of the creation; those great ones obtain the heaven where there are the ancient ages and the gods (7).

XIII

Note: Concluding portion of the Puruṣa-Sūkta. (From 1 to 5).

1. He was born of the waters and the elixirs of the earth; he moved round in the rule of Viśvakarman (causer of the world); Tvaṣṭṛ makes the forms of him; I know that (greatness) of the Puruṣa (person) first.

2. I know this great Puruṣa of the colour of the sun, out of the darkness; he, who knows him thus, becomes immortal here; there is no other way for reaching the (immortality).

3. Prajāpati moves in the womb of the creation; he becomes born in various forms, (though really) being unborn (1). The wise ones know the cause of that; the sages wish the foot of the rays.

4. Salutation to that shining Brahman, who shines for the sake of the gods, who is the priest of the gods and who was born before the gods.

[[P169]]

5. The gods, while producing the lights of the Brahman (consciousness), said in the first time; “who knows thus of Brahman, the gods live according to his wish”.

6. (O Ātman:) the goddess of shyness and the goddess of prosperity are your wives; the days and the nights are the sides; the stars are the forms; Aśvins are the opened (mouth); grant the desired (thing); grant that (heaven); grant all (for me).

XIV

Note: Mantras to be used at the Upahoma after offering the oblations to Death-god. (From 1 to 10).

1. The breath (Prāṇa) holds (the bodies) being held (by Brahman); that one god has entered variously (in the beings); when he becomes lazy, then he sets again leaving the burden of holding the body.

2. (People) say him, indeed, mortal and also immortal, (they) say him holder, (they) say him protector; he holds (the body) being held (by Brahman), who knows this truly for keeping (in the heart).

3. This (Prāṇa) leaves even a new-born one and does not leave even an old one (1); he leaves many in one day; he is an active god to be prayed always.

4. Who knows from where this (breath) was born, and also who knows what relation this has with Brahman; in his old sleeping body this (breath) plays and does not leave in the gone days.

[[P170]]

5. (O Prāṇa!), the waters follow you (i.e., they rain when the Prāṇa wind blows heavily) as the knowing cows follow the calves nourishing by the milk; you burn the fire, the carrier of the oblations; you are the holder Mātariśvan (wind) of the subjects (2).

6. You are the sacrifice, you are, verily, Soma; all the gods come to your invoking. You are one, entered many beings. May salutation be for you; become one to be invoked easily.177

7. O Prāṇa (in-breath) and Apāna (out-breath), coming and going in the body, may salutation be to you both, hear my invoking: I invoke you by the praise; come soon; as young ones leave (kill) him, he hates me.

8. O Prāṇa and Apāna, being concordant, leave (kill) him; do not go with his breath (3); O gods (Prāṇa and Apāna), being concordant by the praise, give him to us for killing, (may) I kill him.

9. The ’non-being’ (Asat) produced; the (Prāṇa breath) was born from that ‘being’; whomever he (Prāṇa) produced, they all became his protector; when he becomes lazy in holding the burden, then leaving the burden he sets away again.

10. Then you became as Prāṇa as the great enjoying of Prajāpati, when you caused to breathe well the nine gods (holes of the body).

XV

Note: The Mṛtyu-Sūkta to be uttered after Brahma-medha sacrifice. (From 1 to 6).

1. The gods (senses) follow the carrying (death), who carries (the breaths), who is the lord of the world and the Bull among the wise ones; this Brahman (hymn) has come in my favour; do not destroy my way; make stride (for killing my enemies).

[[P171]]

2. O Death, do not cut, do not kill; do not destroy my power, do not take away (anything) as thief; O strong one, do not kill my offspring and the span of my life; I serve you, the observer of the men, by the oblation.

3. All the quarters hear well the fierce and trembling Death (1); again they produce by his desire.

4. By the desire of the Death may my desire be fulfilled; by his heart may my heart (come to me); what is favourable of these senses, may that come to me.

5. O Death, follow your other path, which is your other than the path of the gods; I say to you, who has eyes and ears, “Do not kill my offspring and brave ones”.

6. Saluting and praying, I worship the Death, born first of the Ṛta (Settled Order), who is the sprinkler of the beings and the one king of the progeny of the men (2).

XVI

Note: Mantras for Sūrya (sun).

1. O Sūrya (sun), you are moving one, seer of the world and maker of the light; you cause the world shine splendourly; (O fluid of Soma etc.,); you have been taken in the Upāyama (sacrificial vessel); I (sacrifice) you for the shining sun; he (Sun) is your cause; I (sacrifice) you for the shining sun.

[[P172]]

XVII

1. O Soma, the exalted one, be satisfied by all protections; be increased for us.178

XVIII

1. Those men went to the Death, who saw the rising dawns in the old age, we have also to go to (Death) who are seeing (the dawns), and they will also go, who will see the dawns in future.179

XIX

1. (O Fuel), I put you as shining one (on the dead one), I put you as the maker of the light, I put you as the knower of the light, I put you as full of light, I put you as burning, I put you as being burnt with the ‘Malmala’ sounds of the flames, I put you as being shined, I put you as being lighted, I put you as many, I put you as of great light, I put you as awaking one, I put you as waking one.180

[[P173]]

XX

1. Hail! for Prayāsa, Hail! for Āyāsa! Hail! for Viyāsa, Hail! for Saṃyāsa! Hail! for Udyāsa, Hail! for Avayāsa! Hail! for Śuce! Hail! for Śoka! Hail! for Tapyatva, Hail! for Tapat! Hail! for the (sin of) killing of the Brāhmaṇas, Hail! for all.181

XXI

1. (I sacrifice) for (the god) Citta (heart) by progeny; for Bhava by liver (Yakṛt), for Rudra by the flesh of the lower part, for Paśupati (lord of cattle) by the fat heart, for the fire by heart, for Rudra by the blood, for Śarva by the nerves, for Mahādeva (great god) by the inner sides, for Osiṣṭhahana by fleshes (named Śiṃśinikā, i.e., the parts of singing organs).182

I pray for that happiness and welfare for the progress of the sacrifice and for the progress of the sacrificer. May divine welfare be for us. Further may the medicine (remover of all the sufferings) obtain place. May the propitiation be for our two-footed (men). May propitiation be for the four-footed (cattle).

Om Peace! Peace! Peace!

[[P174]]

IV

[[P175]]

I

Section: The Pravargya Sacrifice.

Hariḥ Oṃ.

1. Salutation to the speech, which has been uttered, salutation to that speech which is yet unuttered; salutation to the speech, salutation to the lord of the speech (Vācaspati); salutation to the seers, the makers of the Mantras, the lords of the Mantras; may not the seers, the makers of the Mantras, the lords of the Mantras disobey me; may I not disobey the seers, the makers of the Mantras, the lords of the Mantras.183

2. May I speak the speech which is related to all the gods, auspicious, unbroken and dear to the gods.

3. May the heaven be comfortable for me, may the earth be comfortable for me, may this all world be comfortable for me, may the moon and the sun be comfortable for me, may Brahman and Prajāpati be comfortable for me.

4. I will speak the matter, the world, the light, the austerity, Brahman and the truth.

5. I spread this bed (Pravargya) for that; may there be bed (shelter) for my offspring, cattle; may I become bed (shelter) for my cattle and offspring.

6. May Prāṇa (in-breath) and Apāna (out-breath) protect me; may Prāṇa and Apāna do not leave me.

[[P176]]

7. I will think the honey (Pravargya), I will produce the honey, I will speak the honey; may I speak the honeyful (sweet) speech for the gods, well-hearing for the men (fathers); may the gods protect me as such, may the fathers also please me for the sake of beauty.

Om Peace! Peace! Peace!

II

Section: The construction of the Mahāvīra.

1. The sacrificing priests of the learned, great and wise (sacrificer) concentrate their minds, concentrate their wisdom; Savitṛ, the knower of all the rules, alone, has created this all; great is the fame of the god Savitṛ.184

2. I take you by the impelling of the god Savitṛ, by the arms of Aśvins, by the hands of Pūṣan.185

3. You are Abhri (an instrument), a double-headed hoe of the wood of the Mimosa Catechu and woman, performer of the sacrifice for the gods.186

4. O Brahmaṇaspati, stand up (1), be increased; desirous of divine ones, pray you; may Maruts, well donators go near; O Indra, become swift-going one with us.187

5. May Brahmaṇaspati come well; may the goddess Sūnṛtā (true speech) come well; may the gods lead our sacrifice for obtaining the manly Vīra (Mahāvīra pot), the cooker of the five foods.188

[[P177]]

6. May the goddesses heaven and earth assent me.189

7. May I prosper today, this is the head of the sacrifice (2).190

8. (I take) you for the sacrifice, (I put) you on the head of the sacrifice.191

9. You were this much before.192

10. May I prosper today, this is the head of the sacrifice; (I take) you for the sacrifice, (I put) you on the head of the sacrifice.193

11. O divine white-ants, you are the first-born of this living one, observers of the Ṛta (Settled Order).194

12. May I prosper today; this is the head of the sacrifice (3); (I take) you for the sacrifice; (I put) you on the head of the sacrifice.195

13. You are the vigour of Indra.196

14. May I prosper today; this is the head of the sacrifice; (I take) you for the sacrifice; (I put) you on the head of the sacrifice.

15. You are born of the fire, the semen of Prajāpati.197

16. May I prosper today; this is the head of the sacrifice (4); (I take) you for the sacrifice; (I put) you on the head of the sacrifice.

[[P178]]

17. Put the life, put the Prāṇa-breath, put the Apāna-breath, put the Vyāna-breath, put the eye, put the ears, put the mind, put the speech, put the self, put the firm-standing, put me, put in me.198

18. May the honey make you honeyful.199

19. You are the head of the sacrifice (5).200

20. Be the feet of the sacrifice.201

21. I perform you by the Gāyatrī-verse, I perform you by the Triṣṭubh-verse, I perform you by the Jagatī-verse.202

22. You are the girdle of the sacrifice.

23. May Aditi take your hole by the Paṅkti-verse.

24. Be cooked by the heat of the sun.

25. You are the sacrifice (6).

III

Note: Sanctification of Mahāvīra.

1. You are the issue of the sprinkling horse; may Varuṇa, the holder of the rules heat you by the firm rules of Mitra and Varuṇa.203

[[P179]]

2. I give you for the (flames named) Arciṣ (shining), Śociṣ (burning), Jyotis (light) and Ātapa (heat).204

3. Mitra becomes vast seeing this in the heaven with greatness and in the earth with the fame (1); (all say) the fame of the donation of the god Mitra, the holder of the beings; his donation is of wonderful fame.205

4. (I take) you for the performance.206

5. May the god Savitṛ take you above by the power having good hands, good fingers and good arms.207

6. Fulfill the quarters being not contained on the earth; stand up; be increased (2); stand firm above.208

7. I see you by the eye of the sun for the straight (earth), for the good (mid-region), for the good residence (heaven) and for the prosperity (quarters).209

8. This I make firm that, the son of that with the subjects, cattle and brilliance of sacred knowledge.210

9. I sprinkle you by the Gāyatrī-verse; I sprinkle you by the Triṣṭubh-verse; I sprinkle you by the Jagatī-verse; may the speech sprinkle you, may the vigour sprinkle you, may the oblation sprinkle you; sprinkle the speech; sprinkle the vigour; sprinkle the oblation.211

10. O god of infront, I hang you above (3).212

[[P180]]

IV

1. O Brahman, I perform by the Pravargya (-sacrifice); O Hotṛ (priest), praise well the Gharma (Pravargya); O Āgnīdhra and Rauhiṇa (-priests) put the Puroḍāśa above; O Pratiprasthātṛ (-priest), bring; O Prastotṛ (-priest) sing the Sāmans.

2. May the stobhas (hymns) carry you, who are full of Yajus-Mantras, of satisfied senses by the Sāmans, assented by the all-gods (Viśve-devas) and Maruts; made vast by the Dakṣiṇās (sacrificial fees), taking across and pleasant-minded one; may you put splendour for us being not injured; Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Suvar; perform (the Pravargya, O priests) (1).

V

Note: Exhortations (From 1 to 2). Placing of the Mahāvīra on the fire.

1. O Brahman, we perform (Pravargya); O Hotṛ (-priest), praise the Gharma (Pravargya).213

2. (I sprinkle) you for the rule, for the sacrifice, for the brilliance of the sun.214

3. Hail for Prāṇa-breath! Hail for Vyāna-breath! Hail for Apāna-breath! Hail for the eye! Hail for the ear! Hail for the mind! Hail for Vāc and Sarasvatī! Hail for encouragement! Hail for the will! Hail for the vigour! Hail for the vitality! (1)215

[[P181]]

4. May the god Savitṛ mix you with the honey (1).216

5. Protect the earth from the heat.217

6. You are the ray, you are the shining, you are the light.218

7. Sit well; you are great; shine becoming worth choosing by the gods; O pure and good fire, spread the smoke, which is mild and worth seeing.219

8. Whom the Brāhmaṇas, being heated by the fire, mix (with ghee) as making that vast and as one full of the fat; that Gharma (Pravargya) has come to the fire performing the Ṛta (sacrifice) like the dearest son near his father (2).220

9. (O goddess), not to be injured, in the east, in the rule of the fire, grant me life; as possessor of son, in the south, in the rule of Indra, grant me offspring; sitting well, in the west, in the rule of the god Savitṛ, grant me breath; famous, in the north (3), in the rule of Mitra-Varuṇa, grant me hearing; disposer, above, in the rule of Bṛhaspati, grant me Brahma (sacred knowledge), grant me Kṣatra (dominion), put brilliance in me, put splendour in me, put fame in me, put mind in me.221

10. You are the pervading of Manu (men), having many sons; protect me from all the violences (4); become well enjoyable for me; do not, do not kill.222

[[P182]]

11. O fire, heat (destroy) the inner enemies; heat (destroy) the not praising and coming outer enemy; heat (destroy), O knowing Vasu (giver of residence), those who have no good heart (for us); for them may your (flames), not injured and not tired, stand firm.223

12. You are collected, you are collected well; Hail for Maruts; be spread all over.224

13. You are measuring, you are good measuring, you are each measuring (5), you are well measuring, you are best measuring, you are above measuring, you are the inner holder of the mid-region.225

14. Protect the heaven from the heat.226

15. O Indra (Mahāvīra), being increased by these praises, fulfill that which is little here; when you strike the great clouds for the praisers, may then we be your enjoyers.227

16. One is your white and one is your sacrificeable form (6); of different forms are the days; you are like the heaven; you protect all the measurings, O possessor of food; O Pūṣan (nourisher Mahāvīra), may your gift be auspicious here.

17. O able Rudra (Mahāvīra), you hold the bow and the arrows; O able one, you wear various sacrificeable Niṣka (-ornament); O able one, you show mercy on this water and earth; there is none more powerful than you.

[[P183]]

18. You are of Gāyatrī-verse, of Triṣṭup-verse and of Jagatī-verse.228

19. Honey, honey, honey (7).229

VI

Note: Mantras for sanctifying the Mahāvīra.

1. You shine in the east by ten (rays); you shine in the south by ten (rays); you shine in the west by ten (rays); you shine in the north by ten (rays); you shine above by ten (rays); may you, pleasant-minded and not violated, put brilliance in me.230

2. May the fire, with Vasus, make you shine in the east by the Gāyatrī-verse; you as such make us shine being shined.231

3. May Indra, with Rudras, make you shine in the south by the Triṣṭup-verse; you as such make us shine being shined. May Varuṇa, with Ādityas, make you shine in the west by the Jagatī-verse; you as such make us shine being shined (1). May the heavenly Maruta (wind), with Maruts, make you shine in the north by the Anuṣṭup-verse; may you as such make us shine, being shined. May Bṛhaspati, with all-gods (Viśve-devas), make you shine above by the Paṅkti-verse. May you as such make us shine, being shined.

4. O Gharma (Mahāvīra), you are shining among the gods; may I become shining among the men.232

[[P184]]

5. You are among the gods emperor, shining, full of life, brilliant and having the splendour of the sacred knowledge; among the men may I become shining, full of life, brilliant and having splendour of the sacred knowledge.

6. You are the shining, put the shining in me, (yea) the shining in me.

7. You shine in the east by ten (rays); you shine in the south by ten (rays); you shine in the west by ten (rays); you shine in the north by ten (rays); you shine above by ten (rays), being pleasant-minded one; O emperor, you as such put food and vigour for us; as possessor of the food purifying me, the possessor of the food; the Gharma (Mahāvīra) has been shining by me; may I become shining (2).

VII

Note: Mantras for looking at the Mahāvīra.

1. I have seen the protector (Mahāvīra in the form of the sun), which is not destroyable, moving in the ways infront and backward; that protects the (subjects), gathered or gone in different places; that comes again and again in the worlds.233

2. You protected us here by Madhu (spring season) and Mādhvī (wife of spring), (yea) by Madhu and Madhuci (wife of spring); (I see the Mahāvīra) following you for obtaining the gods.234

3. The fire has gone with the fire, with the god Savitṛ and shines with (the god) sun (1).235

[[P185]]

4. Hail! The fire has gone with the heat, with the god Savitṛ and shined with (the god) Sun.

5. You shine as the holder of the heaven, the region, the holder of the earth, the holder of the vast mid-region, the holder god of the gods, the immortal one and born of the austerity.236

6. (I see you) for the sake of the heart; (I see) you for the sake of the mind; (I see) you for the sake of the heaven; (I see) you for the sake of the sun (2); make this sacrifice above, give these sacrificings to the gods in the heaven.237

7. (I see you) O lord of all the earths, O lord of all the world; O lord of the mind of all; O lord of all the speech, O lord of all the austerity, O lord of all the Brahman (sacred knowledge).238

8. O Gharma (Pravargya), you are famous in the gods; protect the gods.239

9. Grant us the speech, born of the austerity, going to the gods (3).240

10. (This is) the womb of the gods.

11. (This is) the father of the men.

12. (This is) the lord of the subjects.

13. (This is) the wisdom of the poets.

14. This god has done effort with the god Savitṛ and shined with the sun.

15. O Gharma (Mahāvīra), you are giver of life and brilliance to us.

[[P186]]

16. You are our father, impel us as a father.

17. You are the putter of the life, body, milk, brilliance, worship and wealth (4); O fulfiller of the mid-region, you are greater than the vast; may we obtain you, do not, do not kill.

18. O fire, you are the lord of the house of the subjects, consisting of all the men; O youngest one, protect us from the sin by hundred fulfillings (of the wealth etc.); make us sacrificers for hundred winters, make us courageous who (were) lazy (first); may here, indeed, be the wealths.241

19. May I make sexual intercourse full of Tvaṣṭṛ (form-making god) by your protection; having good semen, keeping that semen, may I obtain the brave offspring in your presence; may I not be separated from wealth and nourishing (5).242

VIII

Note: Mantras for pouring milk on the Mahāvīra.

1. I take you by the impelling of the god Savitṛ, by the arms of Aśvins, by the hands of Pūṣan.243

2. You are the bridle for Aditi.244

3. O Iḍā, come; O Aditi, come; O Sarasvatī, come.245

4. O that…..(name of the cow), come; O that…..come; O that…..come (1).

[[P187]]

5. You are the turban of Aditi.246

6. You, the son of Iḍā, are the wind.247

7. May Pūṣan release you; for Aśvins, cause to swell milk.248

8. O Sarasvatī, which is your udder, sleeping, comfortful, by which you nourish all the choosable ones, which keeps wealth, which is knower of the things, good donator; make that worth sucking for the gods.249

9. O calf, leave the Gharma (milk); O calf, protect the Gharma (2); leave for the Gharma (Pravargya).250

10. May Bṛhaspati go to you (for milking you).251

11. (O udders!) you are donators, you are worth sucking by the calf and separated by the blood.252

12. For Aśvins, cause to swell milk; for Sarasvatī cause to swell milk; for Bṛhaspati, cause to swell milk; for Indra, cause to swell milk; (yes) for Indra, cause to swell milk (3).253

13. You are of Gāyatrī-verse, you are of Triṣṭup-verse, you are of Jagatī-verse.254

14. Come to me with the part of the vigour.255

15. O Indra and Aśvins, drink the Gharma (milk with ghee) of the sweet honey.256

[[P188]]

16. Hail! I sacrifice you for the rays of the sun that cause rain.257

17. You are the sweet oblation.258

18. Heat even the heat of the sun.259

19. I take you by the heaven and the earth (4).260

20. I raise you by the mid-region.261

21. May I become able to hold you being assented by the gods and the fathers.262

22. You are the brilliance; follow the brilliance; touching the heaven, do not, do not violate; touching the mid-region, do not, do not violate; touching the earth, do not, do not violate; you are paradise, grant me paradise; grant me heaven, protect me from the heaven (5).263

IX

Note: Sacrificing of the oblations to the Āhavanīya-fire.

1. Hail for you, the wind, the ocean! Hail for you, the wind, the water! Hail for you, the wind, the non-injurable! Hail for you, the wind, the protector! Hail for you, the wind, the moving one! Hail for you, the wind, the active! Hail for you, the fire, full of the things! Hail for you, Soma, accompanied with Rudras! Hail for you, Varuṇa, accompanied with Ādityas (1)! Hail for you, Bṛhaspati, accompanied with all the gods! Hail for you, Savitṛ, accompanied with Ṛbhus, accompanied with the pervading one, accompanied with the lord, accompanied with the food! Hail for you, Yama, accompanied with Aṅgiras, accompanied with the fathers.264

[[P189]]

2. All the good-wishes are (in Brahman), sitting in the south.265

3. (He) worshipped all the gods.266

4. O Aśvins, drink this sweet Gharma (milk with ghee) of which Hail has been done! Hail for the fire, the sacrificeable! May there propitiation be by the Yajus-Mantra.267

5. O Aśvins, drink the Gharma (milk with ghee) which is good for the heart (2); (protect us) by your shining protections (in this sacrificing) day; may the heaven and earth assent you; Hail for Indra!268

6. Hail for Indra! Vāṭ (Hail)!

7. Aśvins drank the Gharma (milk and ghee), which was good for the heart; the heaven and the earth assented you; that thing is as Vāṭ (Hail). Salutation to the heaven, salutation to the mid-region, salutation to the earth (3); put this sacrifice in the heaven; (yea) in the heaven put this sacrifice; go to the heaven, go to the mid-region, go to the earth, go to the five great quarters, go to the gods, who drink the Gharma (milk with ghee), go to the fathers, who drink the Gharma (milk with ghee) (4).269

[[P190]]

X

Note: Agnihotra sacrifice from the remaining articles.

1. Be increased for the food; be increased for the vigour; be increased for Brahman; be increased for the dominion; be increased for the waters; be increased for the plants; be increased for the vegetables; be increased for the heaven and the earth; be increased for the good beings; be increased for the brilliance of the sacred knowledge (1); be increased for the sacrificer; be increased for my greatness.270

2. (I sacrifice) you for the brilliance, for the shining wealth, for the senses and for the prosperity.271

3. You are the holder; may you become my good holder; put the brilliance of the sacred knowledge in us, put the dominion, put the subject; may the wind not move you even (2).272

4. I seat you in his breath, who hates us and whom we hate; go with him useless (making his works useless).273

5. Hail for Pūṣan, the destroyer of the enemies! Hail for …! Hail for the promoting ones! Hail for the stones! Hail for the heaven and the earth! Hail for the fathers, drinkers of the Gharma (milk with ghee)!274

6. Hail for Rudra, the chief Rudra.275

7. May the light of the day be connected with the impelling (of the sun); the good light of the lights; Hail! May the light of the night be connected with the impelling (of the moon); the good light of the lights.276

[[P191]]

8. You have fulfilled (my desires); protect me from the day and the night; this is, O fire, your fuel; be increased by that; give me life; make me full of brilliance. You have fulfilled (my desires); protect me from the night and the day (4); this is, O fire, your fuel; be increased by that; give me life; make me full of brilliance.277

9. The fire is the light; the light is the sun, the sun is the light; the light is the sun; Hail!278

10. Bhūḥ (the earth or the being), Hail! The fire, Hail!279

11. The oblation, the sweet oblation has been sacrificed in the most lordly fire; you are our father; do not, do not kill; I have seen your (essence), O divine Gharma (Pravargya), (the essence of) one full of honey, full of food, full of corn, full of the beauty of the limbs, full of the Svadhā (sacrificial oblation); we have enjoyed you as such; do not, do not kill.280

12. Hail for you! Hail for the rays of the sun; Hail for you! (Hail) for the stars! (5)281

XI

Note: Mantras for taking down the Pravargya.

1. O Gharma (Pravargya), what is your sorrow in the heaven, what is in the Gāyatrī-verse, what is in the Brāhmaṇa, what is in the sacrificial place (Havirdhāna); all that I destroy by this sacrifice.282

[[P192]]

2. O Gharma (Pravargya), what is your sorrow in the mid-region, what is in the Triṣṭup-verse, what is in the Rājanya (ruling caste Kṣatriya), what is in the Āgnīdhra-priest; I destroy all that by this sacrifice, Hail! O Gharma, what is your sorrow on the earth, in the Jagatī-verse, in the Vaiśya (caste); I destroy all that by this sacrifice, Hail!283

3. “May the assent for us today” and “you, indeed, in assent” (these Mantras should be uttered).284285

4. We follow you, the protecting one of the heaven; protect (2) the bodies of the mid-region; we follow you, the holder of the earth for the new fruit.286

5. We follow you, the protector of the Brahman (Brāhmaṇa caste); protect the bodies of the Kṣatra (Kṣatriya caste); we follow you, the holder of the Viśa (Vaiśya caste or the subject) for the new fruit.

6. We follow you, for the protection of the breath; protect the bodies of the eye; I follow you, the holder of the ear for the new fruit.

7. You are the beauty of the propitiation-holding (3), the son of the men-holding one.287

8. You carry the propitiation, you carry (that) from all (the directions).

9. The quadrangular (altar) is the navel of Ṛta (settled order).288

[[P193]]

10. You are the seat; full of all the life and the vast comfort.289

11. We have put away the haters and the wicked ones and obtained the opposite of these by the rule (of this sacrifice).290

12. O Gharma, this is your food, this is your fulfilling; be (4) increased and satisfied by that; may we become increased and satisfied.291

13. You are the divine Gandharva named Ranti, your sacrificing roof is one having the feet; the fire is the president; Rudra is sovereign.292

14. May we be connected with the life, with the breath, with lordship of the cows, with the brilliance, with the milk, with the wealth and the nourishing (5).

15. Who hates us and whom we hate, he has been destroyed.

16. (This Pravargya, as) the sprinkler, the destroyer (of the sins), the great, the observable like Mitra, has shouted and shines with the sun.

17. You are the knower, the cause of the ocean (cloud), the pleasing one, the courageous, the falcon, the observer of Ṛta (Settled Order), having the golden feathers, the bird, the nourisher, sitting firm in the great seat of the gathering (6); may salutation be to you, do not, do not kill.

18. The waters saw Soma-stealing Viśvāvasu Gandharva; they went to him by the Ṛta (Settled Order); Indra also followed them hastily; he saw the encirclings of the sun (for the protection of Soma).

[[P194]]

19. May Viśvāvasu, the divine Gandharva, the maker of the pleasing songs, tell us all that which we have performed (and) which we do not know (7); impelling our wisdoms may he protect our wisdoms. He found the purifying (Soma) in the feet of the rivers; opened the doors of (the rivers), closed by the stones; the Gandharva said about the nectar of these; then Indra knew him not less.

20. O god Gharma (Pravargya), this you obtained the gods.

21. This man, I obtained the men; O drinking of Soma, come to me.

22. May the waters and the plants be good friends of us; may they become bad friends of him, who hates me or whom I hate.293

23. “May we, above the darkness”294295

24. and “Above, that, verily,” “wonderful” (these Mantras should be uttered).296297

25. O fire, proclaim this new (sacrifice), connected with the Gāyatrī-verse for the fruit-giving to us among the gods (8).298

XII

Note: Collection of the milk, honey, and curd in the Mahāvīra pot.

1. You are the milk of the great cows milked for the gods.299

2. You are the shining of the light, the elixir of the vegetables and the plants.

3. We, the possessors of the food, put you, the possessor of the food having a mind for obtaining the high heaven.

[[P195]]

XIII

1. The heaven sprinkled the earth; the young bull sprinkled the cows; all these worlds have been sprinkled; may the sprinkled sacrifice generate.

2. The sprinkled one generated, (yea), generated well; from the sprinkled one the sprinkling one is generated; may we generate from our sprinkled one.

XIV

1. I destroy that lightning, which fell from the east by this your sacrifice; I destroy that lightning, which fell from the south, west, north, or above by this your sacrifice.

XV

1. Hail for Prāṇa-breath! Hail for Apāna-breath! Hail for the eye!

Hail for Vyāna-breath!

Hail!

Hail for the ear! Hail for the mind! Hail for the Vāc! Hail for Sarasvatī!

XVI

Note: Expiations for defects in the Pravargya rite.

1. Hail for Pūṣan, the destroyer of the enemies! Hail for Pūṣan, the good and well way! Hail for Pūṣan, the pleaser of the men! Hail for Pūṣan, full of light! Hail for Pūṣan, the leader of the men; Hail for Pūṣan, full of knowledge!300

[[P196]]

XVII

Note: Consecration of the receptacle of the fire.

1. The sun, rising by the power of this, did not get the trouble and holds all the burden like the earth; may the clean goddess Manīṣā (wisdom) come to us like a well-made, swift chariot. Some, worshipping that, decide that as the great Sāman; they held the sun by that; by that they made the sun shine; what is this Gharma (Pravargya), the head (of the sacrifice), that is this fire. You are the fulfilling, may that (Pravargya) be dear with offspring and cattle; may Prajāpati put you with that god; sit firm with Aṅgiras.

XVIII

Note: Prayer to Agni.

1. O Fire, what are your wet causes (fuel) and what are your dried causes (fuels as nest); what are your prosperous causes and what are your novel causes and what is, O Fire, your body named vigour; by those two being concordant sit here with offspring and wealth (for us). May Prajāpati put you with that god; sit firm with Aṅgiras.301

XIX

Note: Consecration of the receptacle of the fire.

1. You are Agni (fire); you are Vaiśvānara (fire, belonging to all men); you are Saṁvatsara (year, the first in the cycle of six years); you are Parivatsara (the second in the cycle of six years); you are Idāvatsara (the third year); you are Iḍuvatsara (the fourth year); you are Idvatsara (the fifth year); you are Vatsara (the sixth year of the cycle of the six years); of you, as such, the spring season is the head; the summer, the right wing; the rains, the tail; the autumn, the left wing; the dewy season, the middle body; the first wings are the collections (of the fire); the next wings are the Purīṣa (the dung-fuel); the days and the nights are the bricks. Of you, as such, may the months and the half-months be (able for their work); may your seasons be (able for their work); may your year be (able for its work); may your days and nights be (able for their work); A-this, Pra-this, Vi-this, Sam-this, Ud-this — (by these the able-being is specified). May Prajāpati put you with that god; sit firm with Aṅgiras.302

[[P197]]

Bhur, Bhuvah, Suvah.

XX

1. Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Suvaḥ.

2. “Above, for our well-protection”, “Protect us above from…” (these mantras should be uttered).303

3. The old age ate (the Mahāvīra), the rule-maker; respected one, being in the young age; see the greatness of the God that it died today which was similar (to young) yesterday.304

Expiation for the breaking of the Mahāvīra.

[[P198]]

4. Which was broken before by decaying in absence of joining substance; the wealthy Indra will join that having the (joining) articles before him (1); he will perform that broken (sacrifice).305

5. Again with vigour and wealth.306

6. O Gharma, do not trouble us being troubled; do not make our high (work) low; do not bring us to the passion; do not put us, verily, in the darkness; may not the increasing (cruel) persons of Rudra come to us.307

7. O Gharma, do not check us from the good fruit hating us on account of our fault-ful sacrifice; may not Rudra and misfortune check us; and may not the heaven and the earth check us. (2)

8. O Mitra-Varuṇa, protect us here coming near us; O friends, make us shining here respectively; may the good-binding weapon of Ādityas, which is violent, touching hundred ones and poisonous, protect us from every side.

9. “O Varuṇa, my this”, “That I beg you”, “You for us, O Fire”, “O Fire, you as such for us”, “O Fire, you are obtainable”308 (these Mantras should be uttered).

10. “May we, above the darkness”,309 “Above, that wonderful”, “Good-feathered birds”310 (these Mantras should be uttered). (3)311

[[P199]]

XXI

1. Bhūḥ, Bhuvaḥ, Suvaḥ. Put that great sense-organ in me; courage in me; the power in me; put good vigour in me; may the Gharma shine in three ways for me with will and mind, with food and light, with sacrifice and milk, with Brahman and brilliance, with Kṣatra and fame, with truth and austerity; may we enjoy the milking of that (Gharma); may we enjoy the comfort of that; may we enjoy the food of that; may we, being assented, drink your juice, which has been drunk by Indra first, assented and full of honey.312

XXII

Note: Addresses to the fire.

1. O Fire, what are your cruel forms — hunger and thirst, not making out and not eating, great hunger and great thirst, laziness and ignorance — these are, O Fire, your cruel form; by these go to him, who hates us and whom we hate.313

XXIII

Note: Addresses to the fire.

1. Longing, longing for improper things and moisture, hot and cool, cruel and fierceful, freezing, laziness and absence of food — these are, O Fire, your cruel forms; by these go to him, who hates us and whom we hate.314

[[P200]]

Note: Naming of the cooking pans by the names of Maruts.

XXV

1. Ugra, Dhuni, Dhvanta, Dhvena, Dhvanayen, Sebeṣvan, Sehemana, Sahīyān, Etya, Pretya, Vikṣipa (these are the names of Maruts).

XXVIII

1. O Indra, moving set free the cows to move; make us, the last ones, possessors of the cattle; punish that ill-wishing one by the thunderbolt, strike on that sleeping one for (taking) his food.

2. O Fire, be concordant with the fire; O death, be concordant with the death; O prosperous one, may salutation be to you.

3. O Fire, salutation to you once, salutation to you twice, salutation to you thrice, salutation to you four times, salutation to you five times, salutation to you ten times, salutation to you hundred times, salutation to you thousand times, salutation to you innumerable times; may salutation be to you; do not, do not kill.

XXIX

1. You are the envoy of Yama and Rudra both sent (here, in the form) having bloody mouth, being unknown on account of being covered by the blood, having the feet like the dog, the envoy of Yama you run as a vulture, having good feathers you enjoy the corpse.

XXX

Note: Expiations if a vulture, jackal, or wolf is seen.

1. O goddess of speech, which is this crying as a jackal; may that cry for my enemy; O Death, take that to the death; may that trouble by the trouble.318

Expiations if the wolf is seen. Expiations if a vulture is seen. Expiations if a jackal is seen.

[[P202]]

XXXI

1. Which attacking ram cries this speech being sent by someone or coming by his own will, may Indra and Fire, being concordant with Brahman, make that speech auspicious for us.

XXXII

1. O long-faced and bad-jawed, do not cry in our right side; if you speak in my right side, then destroy our enemies.

XXXIII

1. The owl has come in this way having golden eye and iron-mouth, the envoy of the demons (Rākṣasas); O Fire, destroy that from here.

XXXIV

Note: Expiation if a man possessed by ghosts speaks.

1. Which you are speaking superhuman speeches, entering the beings, speak that for defeating our enemies; O Death, take them to the death; may they obtain trouble by the trouble; may the Fire be concordant with the Fire.319

[[P203]]

XXXV

1. You fall here spreading your thighs, you shut again and again your left eye; may there not be any disease to anyone.

Expiations if a vulture falls.

XXXVI

Note: Expiations if the worms fall in the oblation.

1. O worm, I kill you by (the help of) Atri, Kaṇva, and Jamadagni; by the sacred knowledge of Viśvāvasu the king of the worms has been killed, also the old one (of the worms) has been killed, also the father, the mother (have died), and then also the fat ones, the little ones, the black ones, the white ones, and the Asetikās (little-living ones) have been killed; all have been killed with the white ones.320

XXXVII

Note: Oblation for killing somebody by imprecation.

[[P204]]

XXXVIII

1. I curse you by sacred knowledge, I curse you by the oath of the sacred knowledge; I see you by the cruel eyes of Bhṛgus; I meditate you by the cruel mind of Āṅgirasas; I injure by the edge of the cruel weapon; O you named such, fall beneath me.

XXXIX

Note: Siṃījavarī-herb should be plucked in the cow-shed of him who is to be killed by imprecation.

1. O Siṃījavarī-herb, trouble (the enemy); O herb, born on his bed, trouble him on the bed; make him enter the mountains; kill there near the rays of the death; destroy that enemy, who hates us or whom we hate, in the time by which the sun rises in the east.322

Note: Imprecation for killing the enemies / Sāma hymn.

Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Suvaḥ; Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Suvaḥ; Bhūr, Bhuvaḥ, Suvaḥ; from the earth may be performed, from the earth may be performed; from the earth may be performed; may happiness be in happiness (in us), may happiness be in happiness (in us), may happiness be in happiness (in us); obtainable thing has been obtained, obtainable thing has been obtained, obtainable thing has been obtained. (O goddess) in us, in us may the light like the sun be.323

XLI

Note: Offering of the Udumbara wood to the fire while making consecration.

1. The earth is the fuel; the fire burned that, that (earth) burned the fire; I burned that (earth), may that, being burnt, burn me (make me shine) with life, brilliance, splendour, prosperity, fame, brilliance of sacred knowledge, food etc., Hail!324

2. The mid-region is the fuel; the wind burned that; that burned the wind; I burned that; may that, being burnt, make me shine with life, brilliance, splendour, prosperity, fame, brilliance of sacred knowledge, food etc., Hail! The heaven is the fuel; the sun burned that; that burned the sun; I burned that; may that, being burnt, make me shine with life, brilliance, splendour, prosperity, fame, brilliance of sacred knowledge and food etc., Hail!325

3. You are my fuel, related to Prajāpati, destroyer of the enemies, killer of the enemy, Hail!

4. O Fire, the lord of the rules, I will follow the rules; (3) may I do so; may that be increased. O wind, the lord of the rules, O Āditya, the lord of the rules, O Vratapati (lord of the rules), the ruling lord of the rules, I will follow the rules; may I do so; may that be increased.326

5. The heaven is the fuel; the sun burned that; that burned the sun; I burned that; may that, being burnt, make me shine with life, brilliance327, splendour, prosperity, fame, brilliance of sacred knowledge, food etc., Hail! O wind, the lord of the rules, O fire, the lord of the rules, O Vratapati (lord of the rules), the ruling lord of the rules, I followed the rules; I could follow that, that was increased for me (7).330

[[P206]]

XLII

1. May the wind, living in the mid-region, blow being auspicious for us; may the sun shine being auspicious for us; may the days be auspicious for us; may the night be performed auspicious for us.

2. May the dawn come being auspicious for us; may the sun shine being auspicious for us; O well merciful Sarasvatī, be auspicious and most wealful for us; may we not see any lack being in your view.331

[[P207]]

3. (O earth!) you are the residence for Iḍā (food), all possess residence by you; may we become the possessors of the residences; may he become without residence, who hates us and whom we hate.

4. You are the firm-standing; may we become the possessors of the firm-standing; may we not be cut from the firm-standing; may he become without any firm-standing, who hates us and whom we hate.

5. O wind, bring the medicine, remove away the sin; you are the possessor of all the medicines; you move as the envoy of the gods.

6. These two winds blow from the ocean to the distant lands (1); may one bring us courage and may the other remove away whatever is sin.

7. O wind, which is that nectar put in your home, from that give us for living, from that put in us as medicine and from that carry greatness for us.

8. May the wind blow as medicine being auspicious and comfortable for our heart; may that increase our lives.

9. You are the home of Indra; I obtain you with cows, with horses and with that, whatever is ours.

10. I obtain Bhūr (earth); I obtain Bhuvaḥ (mid-region); I obtain Suvaḥ (heaven); I obtain the wind; I obtain the untroubled god; I obtain the unbreakable stone; I obtain Brahma-Kośa (sheath of Brahman) of Prajāpati; I obtain Brahman; I obtain OṂ.

11. May the mid-region be vast for us; (in that) may the fires and the mountains be great; by which welfare the wind makes welfare for me, by that may my inner welfare be.

[[P208]]

12. May Prāṇa-breath and Apāna-breath protect me from the death; may Prāṇa and Apāna not leave me.

13. May the fire put wisdom in me, offspring in me, brilliance in me; may Indra put wisdom in me, offspring in me, sense-organ in me; may the sun put wisdom in me, offspring in me and the shining in me.332

14. O Aśvins, protect us in the days and the nights by the propitiation and good fortune; may Mitra, Varuṇa, Aditi, seas, earth, and heaven please me.

15. By the protection of Prajāpati has been performed this wonderful (sacrifice), a friend, covered by the protection of Prajāpati.

16. May Prajāpati, the true and best increaser of the happiness and food, make you happy; may he make the wealth firm for destroying the evils.

17. Become the good protector of us, the praisers and the friends by your hundred protections.

18. The seers (in the form of) the good-feathered birds, favouring the wisdoms, went to Indra begging: Remove away the darkness; fulfill the eye; set us free who are bound as treasure.

19. May the goddesses, waters, be for our propitiation and drinking, may they flow being auspicious and destroyers of the troubles.

20. I ask the medicine from the waters which are able to grant choosable things and the residences of the moving ones.

21. May the waters and the plants be good friends of us; may they become bad friends of those who hate us and whom we hate.

[[P209]]

22. O Waters, you are full of comforts; may you as such put us in the vigour for seeing the great beautiful ones.

23. What is your most auspicious elixir, make us shareholders of that like the wishing mothers.

24. May we obtain well that elixir by the residence of which you become pleased (4); O Waters, make us powerful to generate.

25. The earth is peaceful, it has become peaceful by the fire, may that destroy my sorrow being peaceful.

26. The mid-region is peaceful, that has become peaceful by the wind; may that destroy my sorrow being peaceful. The heaven is peaceful, that is peaceful by the sun; may that destroy my sorrow being peaceful.

27. The earth is the peace, the mid-region is the peace, the heaven is the peace, the quarters are the peace, the intermediate quarters are the peace, the fire is the peace, the wind is the peace, the sun is the peace, the moon is the peace, the stars are the peace, the waters are the peace, the plants are the peace, the cow is the peace, the goat is the peace, the horse is the peace, the man is the peace, Brahma is the peace, the Brāhmaṇa is the peace, the peace is the peace, may the peace be for me, the peace.

28. I make peace by that peace, the all-peace for my men and cattle; may the peace be for me, the peace.

29. May the prosperity, shyness, holding power, austerity, wisdom, firm-standing, faith, truth, religion, these stand following me when I stand; may not the prosperity, shyness, holding power, austerity, wisdom, firm-standing, faith, truth, religion, these leave me.

[[P210]]

30. May I stand up by life, by my own life, by the elixir of the plants and also by the power of the rain for the immortality.

31. May we see for hundred autumns that eye (orb of the sun), rising brilliantly, assigned by the gods; may we live for hundred autumns; may we be happy for hundred autumns, may we be delighted for hundred autumns, may we be for hundred autumns, may we hear for hundred autumns, may we speak well for hundred autumns, may we be not defeated for hundred autumns, may we be able to see the sun for long time.

32. May that sprinkling sun purify me, which has come from the great ocean from the midst of the waters, who is of red eyes and wise.

33. (O Veda!) you are the flowing of Brahman, you are the two pins of Brahman, you are the holding place of Brahman; by this Brahman this great earth has been held, by this great mid-region has been held; this held the great heaven full of the gods; may I hold that which I know; may the Veda not be forgotten by me.

34. May the wisdom and the thinking power enter me; for accepting the past and future may I live the whole span of life; may I live the whole span of life.

35. By these praising songs fulfill that which is our little (limb). O Indra, when you break the great clouds for the sake of the praisers, may then we be your great share-holders.

36. I have proclaimed Brahma; may not that leave me.333

Om Peace, Peace, Peace.

[[P211]]

Salutation to the speech, which has been uttered; salutation to that speech which is yet unuttered; salutation to the speech, salutation to the lord of the speech (Vācaspati), salutation to the seers, the makers of the Mantras, the lords of the Mantras; may not the seers, the makers of the Mantras, the lords of the Mantras disobey me; may I not disobey the seers, the makers of the Mantra, the lords of the Mantras. May I speak the speech which is related to all the gods, auspicious, unbroken and dear to the gods. May the heaven be comfortable for me, may the earth be comfortable for me, may this all world be comfortable for me, may the moon and the sun be comfortable for me, may Brahman and Prajāpati be comfortable for me. I will speak the matter, the world, the light, the austerity, Brahman and the truth. I spread this bed for that; may there be bed (shelter) for my offspring, cattle; may I become bed (shelter) for my cattle and offspring. May Prāṇa-breath and Apāna-breath protect me from the death; may Prāṇa and Apana not leave me. I will think the honey, I will produce the honey, may I speak the honeyful (sweet) speech for the gods, well-hearing for the men (fathers); may the gods protect me as such; may the fathers also please me for the sake of beauty.

Om Peace!

Peace!

Peace!

[[P212]]

V

Note: Brāhmaṇas of the Mantras of the Prapāṭhaka IV.334

[[P213]]

I

Note: An anecdote about the Pravargya.

Auspicious for us… may that not leave us. Om Peace! Peace! Peace!335

1. The gods began a prosperous Satra (a great Soma-sacrifice or session) desirous of fame. They said, “What fame Kurukṣetra has comes to us first, may that be of all us together.” Their altar, Khāṇḍava was the right half of that; Tūrghna was the left half, Pariṇat was the hinder part, the Maru-country was the (middle part); of them the Utkara (anything dug out for throwing grass etc.) (1); sacrifice of Viṣṇu obtained the fame.336

2. Viṣṇu wished that very much (not to distribute among the gods); he escaped away with that; the gods followed him to check the fame; of him, being followed, the bow was born from the left and the arrows were born from the right; therefore, arrow and bow have pious birth, for they are born of the sacrifice (2); many gods could not defeat Viṣṇu, who was alone; therefore, many men, having no arrow and bow do not defeat one who has arrow and bow.

3. He laughed, “Many gods could not defeat me alone”; from him, laughing in pride, the brilliance ran away; the gods hided that in the plants; they became the Śyāmāka-plants having the name Smayakas (caused by the laughter) (3); therefore, Smayakatva (etymological essence of Smayaka) is of the Smayaka-plants; therefore, a consecrated one should laugh covering his mouth by the hands for holding the brilliance.

[[P214]]

4. He stood holding firmly his bow on the earth.
